**Branch / starting commit:** `codex/runtime-adapters` from clean `f0f6831` on draft PR #7; the latest PR #7 fixture CI and GitGuardian checks passed before branching. Training rows cover all 24 fixed metadata titles; four evaluation users are disjoint from the fit cohort. `ml/split_first_new_user_export.py` fits the previously validation-selected `graph-two-epochs` candidate through the M5.2 route, then emits only item factors/biases, global mean, train-present catalog, positive train pairs, aggregate warm validation, and hashes. The TypeScript evaluator parses the existing compact model contract and calls `preferenceFromHistory`, `buildModelRecommendationsForPreferences`, `createCandidateEligibilityPolicy`, `rankEligibleCandidates`, and the final franchise selector. Nested 1/3/5/10 rating prefixes use a score-independent hash order. It reports model-only ranks separately from the actual displayed graph/catalog fallback and from warm-user factor results. The fit-user test partition was not scored.

**Invented validation measurements:** All four new users have two eligible positive labels in each slice. At 1, 3, 5, and 10 supplied ratings, model-only Hit@10 is `0.75 / 1 / 1 / 1`; Recall@10 is `0.375 / 0.5 / 0.5 / 0.875`; NDCG@10 is `0.3011 / 0.3706 / 0.3706 / 0.5775`; and reciprocal rank is `0.4107 / 0.4792 / 0.4792 / 0.6125`. The corresponding nonseen vector signals map `3/3, 9/9, 16/16, 32/32`; the remaining supplied ratings are Seen and exclude without fitting a vector. One Seen-only first-rating case has zero model candidates and takes the labeled catalog coverage fallback; it contributes a model-only miss. Warm-user validation is reported separately: six eligible fit users, eight positive labels, three top-10 hits, mean Recall@10 `0.4167` and NDCG@10 `0.2544` under a train-items-masked policy without browser filters. The cohort and model are tiny and authored; these values are engineering checks, not representative quality estimates or a reason to tune the fixed candidate.

## Session 2026-09-28 — M6.6 local profile backup and recovery

**Branch / start:** `codex/m6-profile-backup` from clean `b158828f7ebe107f1aff78e508d340b4e055eb32`, draft PR #60's final head. [Fixture CI 36478552396](https://github.com/OptimumAF/WhatAnimeShouldIWatch/actions/runs/36478552396) and GitGuardian passed on that base. The user-provided Crunchyroll page context was not read as a fixture or transmitted.

**Finding and implementation:** The browser had v5 migration and raw backups but no complete portable state/profile backup, previewed merge/replace, reset, or repair control. [Decision 0040](decisions/0040-local-profile-backup.md) records a strict version 1 local JSON contract, 8 MiB file bound, local-conflict-wins merge, explicit replacement losses, and exact original-key journaling across the two current keys and their touched backups. Rejected or interrupted writes roll back; a pending journal supplies original reads while storage refuses recovery. Reset writes valid empty v5 keys without reviving untouched v1/v4 sources; a readable backup repairs corrupt current copies while preserving their raw bytes. The UI keeps unknown catalog IDs and native history details, invalidates stale previews/file reads, and reports storage failure. A full-suite regression exposed controls becoming visible before local graph loading and event handler registration; a loading status now gates interaction until ready while required-data errors remain visible.

## Session 2026-09-28 — M6.4 filter completeness

**Finding and implementation:** The eligibility policy considered the full ranked source, but filtered normal mode hydrated only the leading 30 IDs inside a 100-ID slice. Exploration's metadata controls stopped after the first 100 structurally eligible IDs. A valid lower title could be absent while the interface declared no match. The new pure coverage helper counts all unique structurally eligible anime IDs and separates ready, unchecked, failed, and unavailable outcomes. Normal filtering keeps a bounded initial check and exposes an explicit next-30/retry control through the existing scheduler and abort signal. Exploration advances in explicit 12-ID batches across the full eligible catalog. Sparse shared-genre fallback can remain pending while catalog metadata is unchecked, then rebuilds from newly checked genres. A source with unresolved filter metadata does not prematurely fall through to graph or catalog coverage. Required filters still exclude unknown fields, and both result and empty states identify partial versus completed scans. A terminal 404 is counted as unavailable; transient failure remains retryable. Ranking scores/order, central eligibility, and the provider scheduler did not change.

## Session 2026-09-28 — M6.7 accessible and mobile interaction

**Finding and implementation:** The explorer made every SVG circle a Tab stop and had no list alternative. A 55-node invented graph exposed this path. The SVG remains a pointer view; a labelled disclosure now lists every currently rendered node, with searchable text, 15-item pages, selected state, and a live count. Keyboard activation preserves the list button's focus, announces the selected label/ID, and updates the existing inspection panel. Node search text and sort order are computed when the graph changes, rather than on each keystroke. The command palette now labels its input, keeps focus inside while open, and returns focus to the opener on dismissal; navigation commands move focus to the visible destination nav button. A desktop-to-compact resize moves focus from hidden network controls to their reveal button. The active view has `aria-current`. At phone widths, main controls have 44 CSS-pixel targets, the app grid avoids 320-pixel overflow, and the tall top bar scrolls away so it does not occupy the reading viewport.

## Session 2026-10-05 — M7.1 reproducible browser performance baseline

**Branch / start:** `codex/m7-performance-baseline-stacked` from clean PR #68 head `258d62c2f685ef2487adbf99a927186308f2e310`; PR #68 remains draft and open. An initial M7 work branch started at clean `origin/master` `df7aca6`, but its plan lacked PR #68's newer M6.8 history, so the uncommitted changes were stashed and applied without conflict to this stacked branch before editing the plan. Read root `AGENTS.md`, current plan/latest M6.8 handoff, branch/commit/local changes, and relevant loader, recommendation, graph-render, and browser tests. There are no nested `AGENTS.md` files. The user's Crunchyroll page was not used as a benchmark input.

**Finding and implementation:** No repeatable browser baseline captured byte delivery, parse/index costs, recommendation update time, main-thread stalls, graph rendering, and memory. `web/src/local-performance.ts` now records fixed, opt-in `?perf=1` marks locally; no names contain user data, and no marks are persisted or sent. The loader marks invented catalog/graph/explorer/model JSON body/decode and schema work; the app marks graph/model indexing, complete recommendation updates, and network render JS. `web/bench/browser-performance.mjs` builds only from `fixtures/synthetic-input.json`, serves the built demo, blocks all external requests with a local rejecting proxy, and uses CDP/Resource Timing/Long Tasks/heap metrics. It writes raw samples to ignored `web/test-results/performance-baseline.json`. The benchmark's first request-routing version disabled HTTP caching; the proxy replacement restored a genuine warm reload. A later review moved the favorite after that reload so cold and warm first views use the same empty state. A direct script run after the Pages test correctly rejected a non-demo build; the documented command always rebuilds demo mode first.

**Declared method:** Headless Chromium 153.0.8010.12 on Windows 10.0.26300, Intel i7-12700K, 20 logical CPUs, 64 GiB RAM. Desktop is 1365×768 CSS pixels, DPR 1, unthrottled CPU/network. Mobile is a 390×844 CSS-pixel emulation, DPR 3, 4× CPU slowdown, 150 ms latency, 1.6 Mbps downstream and 750 kbps upstream. Three fresh contexts/profile load an empty-state page with cache disabled, then reload the same empty state with cache enabled. Each run then adds invented title Copper Comet and performs eight toggles per graph/model/hybrid engine, followed by one first network render and three re-renders. Thus each engine has 24 warm-update samples and each profile has 12 graph-render samples. Medians and nearest-rank p95s describe this small local sample, not a service-level target. The first-view clock ends when the initial recommendation DOM update finishes, before browser paint. `response.json()` timing includes remaining body delivery/decode; separate schema and index marks isolate those JS stages. Wire bytes include protocol overhead, encoded/decoded bodies come from Resource Timing, and on-disk gzip is an independent size estimate. JS heap is an instantaneous CDP snapshot, not process RSS.

| Measurement | Desktop | Mobile emulation |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Cold first recommendation DOM, median / p95 | 66.5 / 84.4 ms | 1255.3 / 1263.1 ms |
| Warm same-state reload, median / p95 | 29.2 / 37.5 ms | 770.5 / 781.6 ms |
| Cold / warm HTTP wire bytes, median | 83,459 / 739 B | 83,459 / 739 B |
| Cold encoded / decoded response bodies | 81,786 / 306,918 B | 81,786 / 306,918 B |
| Catalog JSON, schema; graph JSON, schema; graph index, median | 0.6, 0.3; 0.3, 0.5; 0.2 ms | 2.7, 2.2; 3.0, 5.2; 1.5 ms |
| Deferred model JSON, schema, index; explorer JSON, schema, median | 0.3, 0.3, 0.0; 0.4, 0.1 ms | 2.7, 1.9, 0.3; 2.2, 0.6 ms |
| Warm graph / model / hybrid update p95 | 0.9 / 1.4 / 1.8 ms | 5.9 / 9.8 / 11.2 ms |
| First network navigation; render JS p95 | 126.0; 8.8 ms | 305.7; 52.4 ms |
| Cold / post-network JS heap p95 | 3.23 / 7.71 MB | 3.48 / 8.48 MB |
| ≥50 ms main-thread tasks: cold / warm / ranking / network, totals across three runs | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 6 / 0 / 0 / 7 |

The five initial built assets total 306,918 plain bytes or 81,299 bytes when independently gzipped; model and explorer raise the visited asset inventory to 310,621 plain / 82,726 gzip bytes. The preview actually served gzip for the relevant bodies. The local proxy rejected six external requests per profile across the three runs; no provider or proxy service received a username or history. This invented eight-title graph is too small to justify M7.5 workers or M7.7 production-scale budgets.

The table is one complete three-run local sample. A repeat using clean implementation commit `962aff284314fcdb2b2852f6e3658e486ceece27` reported `workingTreeDirty: false` and the same 83,459 cold / 739 warm wire bytes and 81,786 encoded / 306,918 decoded cold body bytes. Its desktop/mobile first-view p95s were 64.5/1240.2 ms; warm graph/model/hybrid update p95s were 0.6/4.7, 0.9/7.0, and 1.0/8.4 ms, respectively. The variation reinforces that three runs on this small fixture are a reproducible diagnostic, not a stable product budget.

No artifact contract, ranking semantics, provider scheduling, or persisted state changed.

**Commands / actual results:** `npm run bench:browser:fixture` regenerated and verified the seven-user/eight-anime/11-pair invented fixture, built the demo, and completed the declared three-run desktop/mobile benchmark. ## Session 2026-10-05 — M7.6 compressed browser delivery

**Branch / start:** `codex/m7-compressed-delivery` from clean `fea1862` (draft PR #72's documentation head), after reading root `AGENTS.md`, M7.6, the latest M7.4 handoff, the actual branch/commit/local changes, the browser loader/runtime contracts, release installer limits, and existing unit/browser release tests. No nested instructions or starting changes. The user's Crunchyroll page supplied no asset or test input.

**Finding and implementation:** The normal-mode legacy loader tried `.gz` but swallowed any gzip parse/HTTP error and silently tried plain JSON. It used `Response(stream).text()` without a decoded-size bound. `web/src/artifact-loader.ts` now reads gzip, browser-decoded JSON, and plain JSON through bounded byte streams, using the release contract's provisional 64 MiB compressed and 256 MiB exposed decoded/plain limits. A test-only limit override can tighten, never raise, those caps. The loader falls back to plain only when gzip returns 404 or the browser lacks usable `DecompressionStream`; a gzip-only host without that capability receives a specific missing-fallback error. Present malformed gzip, bad JSON/schema, and HTTP errors fail with the affected file/status, while transport exceptions remain generic for privacy. Legacy requests use `cache: no-store`. The hash-bound versioned manifest and asset reads retry a mismatched cached response once with `no-store`, then fail closed without borrowing a legacy file. The installer still validates its own transport bytes and stores plain, pinned assets; its contract and approval gates did not change. README documents the compatibility and limits.

**Affected files:** `web/src/artifact-loader.ts`, `web/test/adapters.test.ts`, new `web/tests/compressed-delivery.spec.ts`, `README.md`, the existing root `AGENTS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`, and this record. The inherited M7.4 clean-checkout fix also changed `web/src/style.css` and `web/tests/network-exploration.spec.ts` on parent branch `codex/m7-bounded-exploration`; it was rebased below this slice.

**Commands / actual results:** Initial `npm run data:fixture:check` verified seven invented users/eight anime/11 pairs; pre-edit `npm run test:unit --workspace web` passed 135/135. The new direct adapter cases passed 27/27 after correcting a malformed-gzip error label and a stream-cancellation hang exposed by the small-limit bomb case. A first browser decode test showed that Playwright's route fulfillment exposed raw gzip despite a `Content-Encoding` header; the test now explicitly supplies the browser-exposed decoded body, then passed 4/4. The first full browser run passed 134/135; the existing explorer privacy test caught a loader-authored transport message. After restoring a generic error for transport exceptions, focused release/compression browser cases passed 15/15. Final `npm run data:fixture:check` and `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm test` passed 118 pipeline and 144 web unit cases. The final `npm run test:e2e` passed 135/135 invented/mock browser cases, and `npm run test:pages` passed one built project-path case. Unit tests exercise gzip-only graph/explorer/model, 404 plain fallback, unsupported decompression, exposed decoded JSON, malformed gzip and schema fields, gzip/plain HTTP errors, stale manifest/asset recovery and persistent refusal, advertised and actual size limits, and a compressed small-limit bomb. Browser cases block other external traffic and use no real username or viewing history.

**Parent PR #72 clean-checkout finding:** Its initial [fixture CI 37368451257](https://github.com/OptimumAF/WhatAnimeShouldIWatch/actions/runs/37368451257) passed 130/131 browser cases but found document overflow at 320 CSS pixels on Linux in the M7.4 network case. Ten focused network/accessibility browser cases passed locally. The parent [PR #72 rerun 37370192368](https://github.com/OptimumAF/WhatAnimeShouldIWatch/actions/runs/37370192368) remains queued and still needs its own final result before merge.

**Review / PR:** The staged implementation and documentation diffs and the exact stacked branch diff passed `git diff --check`. Implementation commit `9576e08` (rebased from local `3a746fa`), documentation/type-error commit `1a72353124a27e5acf99bf6f648e08339d59dce5`, and PR-link follow-up `397299c68008653021c3c5b2e837182e033e18e4` are in [draft PR #73](https://github.com/OptimumAF/WhatAnimeShouldIWatch/pull/73), stacked on PR #72's `f9330bb` head. Network render p95 was 298.8 ms, with selection 7.5 ms, construction 68.3 ms, layout 9.5 ms, scope 1.2 ms, and SVG drawing 183.5 ms. The benchmark now reports these durations and long-task overlaps, which are descriptive because one task can overlap multiple nested phases. The SVG renderer groups all selected edge segments by sign, color, width, dash, and selection state into a few pointer-inert paths when more than 500 edges are visible. It retains all selected pairs, their signs and neutral dashes, the same node list, and focused/small-view line rendering. A tenth-viewBox-unit coordinate precision reduces path string size. The pure franchise selector now computes each eligible title's normalized family key and serial flag once, preserving its selection rules.

**Affected files:** `web/bench/browser-performance.mjs`, `web/src/local-performance.ts`, `web/src/main.ts`, `web/src/style.css`, `web/src/franchise-diversity.ts`, `web/tests/network-exploration.spec.ts`, `README.md`, root `AGENTS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`, and this record. Ignored benchmark reports and generated demo assets stay local; no artifact contract, source data, provider call, or public asset changed.

**Verification:** Direct `node --import tsx --test web/test/franchise-diversity.test.ts` passed 6/6; `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm test` passed 118 pipeline and 144 web unit tests. A focused `npx playwright test tests/network-exploration.spec.ts` from `web` passed 2/2; its new invented 600-pair case checks all signed segments/counts, no invalid coordinates, pointer behavior, neutral dashes, 80 nodes, selection dimming, 24 focused lines, reset, and rejected external traffic. `npm run test:e2e` passed 136/136 browser cases and `npm run test:pages` passed its one built project-path case. The final three-run dirty-tree scale diagnostic on the same declared Windows host/Chromium mobile emulation as M7.7 recorded cold first-view p95 2,449 ms, graph/model/hybrid update p95 39.4/49.4/84 ms, franchise phase p95 18.4 ms, network render p95 188 ms, construction 66.7 ms, layout 8.5 ms, SVG 74.6 ms, and post-graph heap about 40.26 MB. It still recorded 77 ranking long tasks (p95 111 ms) and 12 graph long tasks (p95 192 ms). Earlier dirty-tree variants varied substantially; these unpaired samples do not establish a precise causal speedup or finish M7.5. The rejecting benchmark proxy blocked external traffic; no real usernames, histories, or provider data were used.

**Clean-commit benchmark / review:** The 10-file staged diff passed `git diff --cached --check` and was committed as `72806e4d4e6470c3b462e5d7e67141fa75d8f1ec`. `npm run bench:browser:fixture` and `npm run bench:browser:scale` each completed three cold/warm runs per desktop/mobile-emulated profile on that clean commit; both reports recorded `workingTreeDirty: false`, 24 warm updates per engine, and six rejected external requests per profile. ## Session 2026-10-05 — M9.4 bounded background desktop loading

**Branch / start:** `codex/m9-bounded-async-desktop` from clean `a9703276a0ab0513d20af3c0ea8a366e51e15dc6` (draft PR #82's latest head). **Finding / implementation:** The selected v3 manifest and up-to-256-MiB graph still read and parsed synchronously after a blocking native picker. Replaced it with `AsyncFileDialog` and a one-permit Tokio background load queue. It reads in 64-KiB chunks with size and generation cancellation checks, parses/hash-checks the selected files, and prepares the overview outside reactive rendering. Clear, Demo, and a newer selection invalidate old results; a superseded worker releases its permit before the next load. The reactive state retains only summary fields and the prepared display. An initial 94.9-MB invented shape exposed that the former node-first selection showed only one pair among 300 scattered titles. The pair-first bounded heap now scans the exact signed pair evidence, selects at most 300 titles and 1,400 visible pairs, and shortens long visible titles/source/tag labels. It does not rebuild pair statistics or change the v3 contract.

A first PowerShell `npm run ... -- --anime ...` attempt treated flags as npm config and generated the defaults; the documented direct Node command is the custom-size route.

In a repeat, Clear was clicked while Loading was visible; the state became and remained **No data**, with no late graph. The prior node-first build displayed only one pair on the same shape. ## Session 2026-10-05 — M9.5 Windows x64 candidate package

**Branch / start:** `codex/m9-windows-package` from clean `c5e9a629ef7e385c18460729aab0242e2e7838cd` (draft PR #83 final head). Re-read root `AGENTS.md`, M9 plan/latest handoff, decision 0042, actual branch/commit/status, Rust source/lockfile, desktop CI, and package documentation. PR #83's final-head Windows/fixture/GitGuardian checks passed. No nested instructions. No Crunchyroll watch history, username, private rating, or provider request entered this work.

**Dependency and package finding:** Microsoft documents the WebView2 Evergreen Runtime as a machine prerequisite and its registry detection locations; this Rust build statically links the WebView2 loader. Local `dumpbin /dependents` identified `VCRUNTIME140.dll` and `VCRUNTIME140_1.dll` along with Windows system imports. A first PE package check correctly rejected the release binary because it used the console subsystem. The release Rust binary now uses the Windows GUI subsystem, while debug keeps a console. A first package-preflight test found `Get-FileHash` unavailable in a nested Windows PowerShell 5.1 environment despite its availability in the interactive shell; the packaged checker now hashes the EXE with a streamed .NET SHA-256 API.

**Implementation:** Root `rust-toolchain.toml` pins Rust 1.93.1 and rustfmt/Clippy, while the existing `desktop/Cargo.lock` remains committed. The Windows CI uses an action commit plus that exact toolchain, runs locked format/lint/test/build, then creates and checks a portable candidate ZIP without uploading any artifact. The Python standard-library packager accepts only a bounded Windows x64 GUI PE with reviewed normal/delay-load DLL imports and writes a deterministic five-file inventory: EXE, `README.txt`, `Launch.cmd`, `Check-Prerequisites.ps1`, and `package-manifest.json`. ZIP verification bounds compressed/inflated bytes, rejects extra members/metadata/symlinks, recomputes hashes and PE imports, and optionally compares every member to this checkout. The packaged preflight checks the EXE hash, the official WebView2 registry locations, and both x64 VC runtime DLLs; it does not fetch or install either runtime. Microsoft runtimes and datasets are absent from the ZIP. The manifest is a local consistency record, not an authenticated release signature.

**Commands / actual results:** `rustc -vV` reported release 1.93.1 on `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; `rustup show active-toolchain` selected the root pin. `cargo fmt --manifest-path desktop/Cargo.toml -- --check`, `cargo clippy --locked --manifest-path desktop/Cargo.toml --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --locked --manifest-path desktop/Cargo.toml` (9/9), and `cargo build --release --locked --manifest-path desktop/Cargo.toml` passed. `npm run desktop:fixture:check` verified the invented v3 bundle. `python desktop/scripts/windows_package.py package` and `check --compare-source` passed on a 1,803,207-byte local ZIP. Six Python package tests passed, rejecting a changed/truncated EXE, extra asset, hidden ZIP comment, and a changed EXE through the packaged preflight. `dumpbin /headers` showed x64 and Windows GUI subsystem; `/dependents` showed the two VC DLLs and no dynamic WebView2Loader DLL. The five ZIP member names were inspected. After extraction inside ignored `desktop/target/`, `Check-Prerequisites.ps1` found WebView2 154.0.4258.53 and the two VC DLLs. `python` parsed the workflow YAML; `git diff --check` and final review are recorded with the commit below.

**Affected files:** `rust-toolchain.toml`, `.github/workflows/build-rust-desktop.yml`, `desktop/src/main.rs`, new `desktop/package/README.txt`, `Launch.cmd`, and `Check-Prerequisites.ps1`, new `desktop/scripts/windows_package.py` and its six-test suite, `desktop/README.md`, root `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`, and this record. No graph/data/model producer, browser artifact contract, provider transport, public release, or production deployment changed. The ignored ZIP and extracted probe are local only.

## Session 2026-10-05 — M10.1–M10.2 beta preparation

**Branch / start:** `codex/m10-beta-preparation` from clean `e6d6e8915637699072c41a6ba817ab071e8cdcd6` (draft PR #84 final head). **Plan/order decision and deliverable:** Prepared [the beta protocol](BETA_PROTOCOL.md) before human sessions because M0–M8/source-use gates currently hold actual recruitment, data use, and release decisions. The protocol assigns one desktop and one physical-mobile tester to each sparse, broad, niche, and new-to-anime case; gives neutral import-or-select, inspect, filter, correct, save, and return prompts; and requires consent before minimal notes. It excludes raw titles, usernames, lists, ratings, screenshots, and provider responses from default records. It freezes an eight-session decision rule before any session: unassisted journey and correct explanation in at least 6/8 with case coverage, at least three participant-judged plausible unseen titles among the first ten in at least 6/8 fully assessable sessions with case coverage, zero app-marked seen/excluded/watchlist leaks, and stop/fix conditions for crash, loss, unapproved requests, or private-data exposure. An unassessable session cannot silently count as success. The invented eight-title demo cannot validate the ten-title usefulness target.

**Commands / actual results:** `npm run data:fixture:check` passed and verified seven invented users, eight anime, eleven pairs, one duplicate and one unknown input rating. From `web/`, `npx playwright test tests/first-use.spec.ts tests/end-to-end-journeys.spec.ts tests/watchlist.spec.ts` passed **16/16** invented/mocked browser cases, covering manual and browse starts, preview-before-apply import, eligibility/filtering, result save/reload, profile cancellation, backup, and storage rejection. Those automated cases verify that protocol prompts map to existing UI paths; they do not measure human comprehension, plausibility, or the eight-session gate. No new application code or production data changed.

**Affected files / checks not run:** New `docs/BETA_PROTOCOL.md`, updated `docs/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and this record. No human tester was recruited or consented, no feedback was collected, no approved release/catalog was used, and no screenshot or personal history was saved. The full web/Python suite, clean-install audit, physical mobile session, permitted representative bundle, production regeneration, publication, deployment, and hosted checks did not run for this documentation slice.

## Session 2026-10-05 — M2.7 alternate catalog source review (partial)

**Branch / starting evidence:** `codex/m2-catalog-source-candidate` from clean `master` at PR #90 squash merge `60edbd3b685c351d44c0218e3907f924d13f7126`. **Finding / reviewable change:** Official [Wikidata licensing](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Wikidata:Licensing) and [data-access documentation](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Help:Linked_Data_Interface) identify structured data as CC0. The [P4086 external-ID property](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Property:P4086) can potentially map the app's numeric anime IDs without following a MAL link. Official property pages describe potential genre, date, type, episode, duration, classification, and directed-series statements, but do not establish coverage or quality for this catalog. [Commons guidance](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Commons:Reusing_content_outside_Wikimedia/licenses) requires image-level license review. Added [decision 0043](decisions/0043-catalog-source-candidate.md) as a **proposal** for at most 100 owner-supplied non-personal IDs in a restricted local feasibility study. It records exact field/unknown/conflict rules, excludes image and user data, separates metadata identity from ratings identity, and identifies the needed versioned metadata asset. Amended existing decision 0001 and root agent guidance without changing any hold or task acceptance criterion.

## Session 2026-10-05 — M2.7 source-neutral metadata candidate contract (partial)

**Branch / starting evidence:** `codex/m2-metadata-contract` from clean `master` at PR #91 squash merge `645edd213824566306934eafd5ebc2741abfd9d9`. The bounded real Wikidata feasibility study still awaits owner approval and a non-personal ID scope. No source entity or user's browser history was queried.

**Implementation and review:** Added a separate strict `anime-metadata-catalog-v1` candidate parser to `web/src/artifacts.ts`, preserving `anime-catalog-v1` and `release-manifest-v1`. It requires unique sorted anime IDs and source item IDs, bounded title/alias/genre/relation values, explicit nulls for unknown year/format/episode/runtime/classification/score/relation fields, and jurisdiction plus system for a known content classification. It rejects hidden image/user fields and malformed source identity, but the declared source digest is **not** self-verifying. A pure coverage counter compares an explicit ID universe, counting missing entries, known versus usable fields, directed-relation items, and directed targets outside the universe without logging titles or IDs. The contract is not fetched or loaded by the app and is not in a release package. Invented unit and browser cases check accepted unknowns, missing fields, duplicate source IDs, invalid values, hidden user/image fields, and a named field error.
